[100 AD | 400 den | you:2000 hr | sch 0 art 0 | rep 5] > Controlled experiment, hypothesis, replication, publication  [scientific_method]
==============================================================================
tier 0, foundation, confidence C

The hard part is not stating it, it is displacing Aristotelian authority.
Risk is high because the failure mode is cultural, not technical. Multiplier
on every research node thereafter. Contradicting Aristotle and Galen in
public is a social act before it is an intellectual one, and their defenders
hold the chairs, the patronage and the medical guild. Expect organised
opposition, not curiosity. (Needs no standing: writing down zero, or running
a controlled experiment, is something a nobody can do. What standing buys is
being BELIEVED, which is reputation, not a prerequisite.)

tier 1, agriculture, confidence B

Roman throat-and-girth harness chokes a horse under heavy draught. The size

[100 AD | 400 den | you:2000 hr | sch 0 art 0 | rep 5] > help:
  the ledger: money itemises what comes in and what goes out, including where the income comes from
  buy forest: buy forest 100 hectares of coppice, which is where charcoal comes from
  buy mine: buy mine coal 500 tonnes a year of your own workings; it takes years to sink, and it costs to keep standing whether or not you use it. ASK THE PRICE FIRST with quote mine coal 500, and close it with close coal. Materials: coal, iron, copper, lead, tin, silver, gold
  buy slaves: buy slaves 5. This is available because it was the ordinary condition of production in most of these societies, and a model that hides it lies about the cost of everything.
  manumit: buy manumit 5 frees people you hold. They then work better, and it is the decent thing.
  debt: You may spend past what you have, as far as somebody will lend you and no further. Arrears cost interest.

[100 AD | 400 den | you:2000 hr | sch 0 art 0 | rep 5] > help:
  what happens on its own: Some things the engine will do for you if you let it: grow the staff, teach trades, sink mines, buy woodland, shut down what you cannot pay for, pay off a scandal. Every one is a switch you control, and every one can be done by hand instead.
  see them: policy
  change one: policy auto_hire on

[100 AD | 400 den | you:2000 hr | sch 0 art 0 | rep 5] > help:
  eminence: The one hazard no patron, no bribe and no reputation protects you from, because it IS reputation. It rises with how well known you are and how visibly rich, it is multiplied by standing close to the throne, and past the danger line it rolls every year for your ruin. Sejanus was the most protected man in Rome until the morning he was not.
  what lowers it: Nothing lowers it directly, which is the point. What SURVIVES it is a wide, dispersed institution - academy_network makes the hazard itself smaller, and corpus_dispersed means what you know is in too many places to burn. Being merely rich and merely famous is the dangerous combination.
  where to watch it: state shows it under STANDING

[100 AD | 400 den | you:2000 hr | sch 0 art 0 | rep 5] > help:
  risk: What history is about to do to you, with dates, and what you have built that blunts each one. Every hazard is fightable and the numbers are real.
  see it: risk

  auto open: False
  auto shed: False
  auto train: False
changed: (none)
what each does:
  auto hire: grow the staff toward what you can house and pay
  auto buy people: buy slaves when the workshop is short-handed
  auto manumit: free people you hold, over time
  auto train: teach trades this society does not have when a project needs them
  auto mine: sink a mine when a mineral is holding work up
  auto forest: buy coppice when charcoal is holding work up
  auto mothball: stop working mines you cannot pay for
  auto bribe: pay your way out of a scandal before it kills you
  auto shed: let go of WORKS that cost more than they return (this is about buildings and practices, not people)
  auto open: open concerns that plainly pay for themselves. It will NOT open anything whose upkeep exceeds its takings, however much you need it - open those yourself with 'open <id>'
note: Anything switched off here you can still do by hand: hire, train, buy, commission, mothball, restore, bribe.
not policies: Some things are consequences, not automation, and there is no switch for them: people you cannot pay leave, mines you cannot pay for stop being worked once your credit is gone, and creditors take what they are owed. Those follow from having no money, not from a setting.

=================================================
tier 1, rail, confidence C

Hopper wagons eliminate shoveling; load coal or ore, close the chute, and
gravity empties it at the destination. Chutes must be wide enough that cargo
bridges; a stuck chute is worthless. Hinged chutes that open suddenly can
jam if a stone wedges in the opening.

[100 AD | 400 den | you:2000 hr | sch 0 art 0 | rep 5] > policy:
  auto bribe: False
  auto buy people: False
  auto commission: False
  auto forest: False
  auto hire: False
  auto manumit: False
  auto mine: False
  auto mothball: True
  auto open: True
  auto shed: False
  auto train: False
changed:
  auto open: True
what each does:
  auto hire: grow the staff toward what you can house and pay
  auto buy people: buy slaves when the workshop is short-handed
  auto manumit: free people you hold, over time
  auto train: teach trades this society does not have when a project needs them
  auto mine: sink a mine when a mineral is holding work up
  auto forest: buy coppice when charcoal is holding work up
  auto mothball: stop working mines you cannot pay for
  auto bribe: pay your way out of a scandal before it kills you
  auto shed: let go of WORKS that cost more than they return (this is about buildings and practices, not people)
  auto open: open concerns that plainly pay for themselves. It will NOT open anything whose upkeep exceeds its takings, however much you need it - open those yourself with 'open <id>'
note: Anything switched off here you can still do by hand: hire, train, buy, commission, mothball, restore, bribe.
not policies: Some things are consequences, not automation, and there is no switch for them: people you cannot pay leave, mines you cannot pay for stop being worked once your credit is gone, and creditors take what they are owed. Those follow from having no money, not from a setting.

[117 AD | 5446 den | you:2000 hr | sch 0 art 5 | rep 24] > Systematic case hardening, quenching and tempering  [case_hardening]
====================================================================
tier 1, metallurgy, confidence B

Smiths already do all of this in any developed ironworking tradition, but
inconsistently and without a temperature scale. Give them temper colours on
polished steel as a repeatable index and the reject rate collapses. Cheap,
immediate, militarily valuable, entirely unmagical.

COST: 1,356 den total  (360 labour + 435 materials + 800 capital, then x0.85 your civ, x1 distance, x1 scarcity, x1 prices)
YOUR HOURS: 300     CALENDAR FLOOR: 1 years     FAILURE RISK: 12%
STAFF NEEDED: 0 scholars, 2 artisans   (you have 1, 5.1)
HIRED LABOUR: smith 2,000h

  playing across several sittings: Pass --session FILE on the command line. The game is written to that file after every command and read back when you start again, so you do not need to hold a process open or write a script.

[136 AD | 21906 den | you:2000 hr | sch 1 art 3 | rep 67] > help:
  protection: How far your standing shields you when you produce an effect nobody can explain. It decides whether a strange result out of your workshop is read as learning or as sorcery, and it is the only thing money can buy here directly.
  what raises it: A patron, citizenship, a licensed collegium, land endowed in public, a school, and your reputation - and spending on advocacy and piety, which is what `bribe` does when you have no scandal to answer. It caps at 92%.
  what it does NOT protect you from: Eminence. Being too large is the one hazard no protection touches; see help eminence.
  where to watch it: state shows it under STANDING

[136 AD | 21906 den | you:2000 hr | sch 1 art 3 | rep 67] > help:

[136 AD | 21906 den | you:2000 hr | sch 1 art 3 | rep 67] > help:
  labour: You arrive alone. Everything anyone else does for you is hired by the year, bought as a single job, taught by you from nothing if this society has no such trade, or bought outright as a person. Trades are NOT interchangeable: a smith is not a scribe, and a project asking for an engineer cannot be built by smiths however many you have.

    fire: fire smith 1
    train: train machinist 2 - teaches a trade that does not exist here, out of your own hours
    commission: commission smith 400 - buy a job rather than a person

note: Anything switched off here you can still do by hand: hire, train, buy, commission, mothball, restore, bribe.
not policies: Some things are consequences, not automation, and there is no switch for them: people you cannot pay leave, mines you cannot pay for stop being worked once your credit is gone, and creditors take what they are owed. Those follow from having no money, not from a setting.
